# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-04-04_TotalExtraction_18742997.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 10, 2023  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. 
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Segrave, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Roybal and Mr. Laybourn, item #14 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Segrave, 
item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Mr. Laybourn, and pursuant 
to support received by Mr. Segrave and Dr. Aldrich, item #23(b) was removed from the Consent 
Agenda.  Mr. Segrave moved to approve remaining items, seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting 
“no”. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on March 27, 2023.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a bar & grill liquor license application filed with 
the City Clerk’s Office for Ace’s Range LLC d/b/a Ace’s Range, 1505 Pioneer Ave., Cheyenne, 
WY.  Public comment was made by: J Brent Kunz, Ace’s Range. Comment was made by the 
following member of the Governing Body: Dr. Aldrich.  There being no further comments or input, 
and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred 
the application to Finance Committee.   
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a winery permit application filed with the City 
Clerk’s Office for The Blue Stem Wine House, LLC d/b/a The Blue Stem Wine House, 1909 
Warren Ave., Cheyenne, WY.  Public comment was made by: Brandon and Virginia Erdmann, 
The Blue Stem Wine House. Comments were made by the following members of the governing 
body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Roybal. There being no further comments or input, and purpose of the 
hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to 
Finance Committee.   
 
 
 

Mayor Collins opened a public hearing to determine whether compliance of annexation 
conditions required by Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 exist for about 1.29 acres of land at 5224 
Ridge Road (located west of Ridge Road, between Jackson Street and Douglas Street).  Public 
comment was made by: Michael White.  Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Dr. Aldrich and Mr. Roybal.  There being no further comments or input, and 
purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing. 
       
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located west of Southwest Drive south of I-80. Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. 
Laybourn moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried. 
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4497) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County LR Low-Density Residential to LR 
Low-Density Residential for land located west of Southwest Drive south of I-80.  Mr. Laybourn 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – 
all members of the governing body. (#4498) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located west of Ridge Road, between Jackson Street and Douglas Street -- referred to Public 
Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classification of MR Medium-Density Residential for land being 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne located at 5224 Ridge Road (west of Ridge Road, between 
Jackson Street and Douglas Street) -- referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. 
Cook) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from MR Medium-Density Residential to NR-2 
Neighborhood Residential - Medium Density for land located on Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Twin 22 
Subdivision -- referred to Public Services Committee.  (Sponsor – Mr. Cook)   
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Chapter 2.04, Mayor and City Council, within 
Title 2, Administration and Personnel, and Chapter 12.04, Streets, Sidewalks and Driveways 
Generally, within Title 12, Streets, Sidewalks and Public Places, of the Municipal Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to amend provision Governing contract approvals -- referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White) 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Ordinance No. 4429 updating the City of 
Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee Schedule -- referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)      
 
 
RESOLUTION – Providing for the adoption of a Fund Balance Policy in accordance with 
the Governmental Accounting Standard Board (GASB) Statement No. 54. Mr. White moved to 
adopt, seconded by Dr. Aldrich. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. 
Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Cook, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. 

Rinne and Mr. Segrave.  Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: 
Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Mr. Esquibel, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave.  (#6315) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Expressing the will of the Governing Body to consider specified 
Downtown 6th Penny Tax Project Funds for Infrastructure Enhancements for The Children’s 
Museum of Cheyenne.  (2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund). Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
Public comment was made by: Caroline Veit, Board President, Children’s Museum of Cheyenne.  
Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Cook, 
Mr. Johnson and Mr. Laybourn. (#6316) 
 
[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Adopting the Wyoming Region 7 Hazard Mitigation Plan 2023 Update. 
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6317) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Mayor to execute settlement participation forms for the 
City of Cheyenne to participate in the Settlement Agreements designated as the “Teva, Allergan, 
CVS, Walgreens, Walmart National Opioid Settlement” (Revenue to City). Motion (per Consent 
Agenda) carried. (#6318) 
 
[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Reappropriating the Fiscal Year 2023 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 and 16-4-113 -- referred to Finance Committee. 
(Sponsor – Mr. White)   
 
[CA] 
 RESOLUTION – For naming the median which contains the Historic Airport Fountain, 
located to the east of the intersection of Central Avenue and East 8th Avenue, to be known as 
Airport Fountain Park -- referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Laybourn)  
 
[CA]  
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Program Grant to provide the local 
match for operating funds for the City of Cheyenne for its Transit Program -- referred to Finance 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. White)   
          
[CA] 
Business Associate Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Regional 
Medical Center, and Dr. Clair White for the Behavioral Health Co-Response Project.  (Wyoming 
Department of Health Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7729) 
 
[CA] 
Professional Services Contract between the City of Cheyenne and Kyle M. Brandon for 
Public Defender Services (General Fund).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount 
not to exceed $54,000.00). (#7730) 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and The Church of St. Mary 
for Olivet Cemetery grounds maintenance, irrigation, operations and administrative services 
(Revenue to the City).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7731) 
 

[CA] 
Development Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Whitney Ranch 3rd and 4th 
Filing, LLC, to outline responsibilities for public infrastructure improvements relating to Whitney 
Ranch 4th Filing Plat.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#7732) 
 
[CA] 
Agreement for the Purchase, Sale and Exchange of Lands between the City of Cheyenne 
and Sun Valley Estates LLC for the exchange of real property, 15,365 SF, from the City to Sun 
Valley Estates LLC and 15,366 SF, from Sun Valley Estates LLC to the City, both having an 
appraised value of $100,000.00 and generally located south of Raleigh Drive -- referred to Finance 
Committee. 
 
[CA] 
Memorandum of Understanding by and between Laramie County, the City of Cheyenne, 
the Cheyenne Board of Public Utilities, the Cheyenne/Laramie County Board of Health, and the 
Cheyenne Regional Airport Board regarding the management of the ESRI Enterprise Agreement 
(Various City Funds) – referred to Finance Committee.   
 
[CA] 
Telecommunications Right-of-Way License and Use Agreement between the City of 
Cheyenne and AT&T Corp (Revenue to the City) – referred to Finance Committee.   
                  
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7321 for the Development Agreement between the 
City of Cheyenne and Whitney Ranch, LLC, to outline responsibilities for public infrastructure 
improvements related to Whitney Ranch 3rd and 6th Filings.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
(#7321) 
 
[CA] 
Contract Modification #1 to Contract #7625 for the Agreement between the City of 
Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT) for a Wyoming Rural 
Public Transportation grant to assist in providing a local match for operating funds for the Transit 
Program for Fiscal Year 2023 (5311 WYDOT Grant) -- referred to Finance Committee.    
                    
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-17-23 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and S & 
S Builders, LLC for the Pioneer Park Hip Roof Shelter.  (Development Impact Fees & Community 
Development Block Grant) Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (in an amount not to exceed 
$115,415.00). (#7733) 
 
 
Consideration of Bid #E-3-23 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Goat 
Green LLC for Vegetation Management for Crow Creek (Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund & Laramie 
County Weed & Pest Grant). Mr. White moved to approve in the amount of $160,000.00, seconded 
by Mr. Segrave. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Comment 
was made by the following member of the governing body: Mr. Laybourn. (#7734) 
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-16-23 for a City Network Operations Core Layer 3 Switch 
Replacement (2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.                       
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-17-23 for the surplus sale of one (1) used reel grinder and one (1) 
used bedknife grinder (Revenue to the City) – referred to Finance Committee. 
 

[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #E-18-23 for a Professional Services Agreement between the City of 
Cheyenne and Vector Disease Control International (VDCI) for Mosquito Site Testing and 
Mosquito Larvicide Application (Weed & Pest Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.  
 
[CA] 
Consideration of Bid #S-18-23 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and S & 
S Builders, LLC for the Greater Cheyenne Greenway, Greenway Commons Retaining Wall Re-
Construction (2017 and 2021 Specific Purpose Option Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee.    
 
[CA] 
Consideration to Purchase between the City of Cheyenne and Econolite for traffic 
controller cabinets, controllers and conflict monitors for the City of Cheyenne Traffic Division  
(2019-2022 Optional 1% Sales Tax Fund) – referred to Finance Committee. 
      
[CA] 
Request to relocate one dispensing area relating to City of Cheyenne Restaurant Liquor 
License held by Capitol Cinema 16, located at 3410 Concord Rd.,  Cheyenne, Wyoming. Motion 
(per Consent Agenda) carried.  
 
Jim Ridgeway and Mr. Esquibel commented on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
Announcement was made for a Public Hearing to be held April 24, 2023 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for disposal of real property and land exchange between the City for Cheyenne and Sun 
Valley Estates LLC (generally located south of Raleigh Drive).   
 
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 24, 2023 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for a bar and grill application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for FAFO Restaurant 
Group LLC d/b/a Dog Haus Restaurant, 3838 Atkins St., Cheyenne, WY. 
    
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held April 24, 2023 at 6:00 p.m. in 
City Council Chambers, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, WY, and via electronic conference 
meeting, for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action Plan Program Year 2023 prepared by the 
Cheyenne Housing and Community Development office. 
 
Public comment was made by the following individuals under other business: Jim 
Ridgeway, Marlena Schultz, Dog Haus Restaurant, Michael White, and Mead Kelsey. 
 
The following members of the governing body made comments under other business: Dr. 
Aldridge, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White. 
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 7:07 p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
City Clerk 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
April 15, 2023

4O_ Cc,r4MuNI’rY c’ CHcICE
II
MEMO
To:
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council
From:
Jeanie Vetter, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner
Subject:
Public Hearing for Disposal of Real Property and Trade Agreement for land for Greater
Cheyenne Greenway, Sun Valley to Kiwanis Park Connector
Date:
April 5, 2023
1.
The MOU outlining responsibilities for the proposed property exchange with Sun Valley Estates
LLC, Contract #7642, was approved by Council and signed by the Mayor on November 29, 2022.
Since this time, all responsibilities of both the city and the Developer have been met.
2.
The plat for this project was approved by Council on February 27, 2023, and is awaiting
recordation based on Council’s approval of this agreement.
3.
Pursuant to Wyoming Statute 15-1-112, staff has completed all steps associated with the
disposal of city-owned real property for the purposes of trading property for a future Greenway
corridor and trail. City staff has reviewed and approved the associated property as outlined in
the City of Cheyenne checklist for disposal/trade of real property.
4.
Pursuant to Wyoming Statute 15-1-112(b)(ii) An advertisement of the proposed trade has been
published for three (3) weeks in the local newspaper. March 11th, 18th and 25th. An
announcement of a public hearing is included on this agenda for tonight’s City Council meeting,
for public comment.
5.
The trade/sale agreement has been reviewed by all interested parties. The item will be placed
on the Finance Committee agenda for Monday, April 17th for further recommendation and final
reading for approval by the governing body will take place on Monday, April 24th. Once
approved by the governing body and signed by all interested parties, the process shall proceed
to closing for final execution.
Thank you in advance for your consideration. If you have any question, please feel free to contact me.
Respectfully,
Jeanie Vetter
Enclosures:
Land Swap Purchase and Sale Agreement
Exhibit A Land Description Exhibit for City of Cheyenne Greenway Parcel Land Swap
Sun Valley to Kiwanis Park Connector Exhibit
I

TERMS AND CONDITIONS
ARTICLE 1. PREMISES
1.1
Identification of Premises.
In consideration of the rents, covenants, and agreements
contained herein, Landlord leases to Tenant and Tenant leases from Landlord that certain restaurant located
at Lot 8, Block 1, North College Drive, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as more particularly identified on Exhibit A
(the “Premises”).
The building in which the Premises are located (and constituting a portion of the
Premises), with the street address set forth on Exhibit A attached hereto, is sometimes referred to
hereinafter as the “Building.”
1.2
Size. The parties estimate that the Building consists ofa floor area ofapproximately 2,828
rentable square feet as generally set forth on the floor plan attached hereto as Exhibit B.
ARTICLE 2. TERM AND USE
2.1
Rent Commencement Date and Term. The initial term (the “Initial Term”) of this Lease
shall be for a period of 20 Lease Years (defined below) beginning on the Rent Commencement Date and
ending at midnight on the last day ofthe last full Lease Year in the Term. For purposes ofthis Lease, “Rent
Commencement Date” means the date that is the earlier of (i) ten (10) days after issuance of a certificate
of occupancy to Tenant for the Premises, and (ii) the date Tenant opens for business.
For purposes ofthis
Lease, “Lease Month” means
each
consecutive
one-month period commencing on
the Rent
Commencement Date, and a “Lease Year” means a period of 12 Lease Months.
2.2
Renewal Options. Provided Tenant is not in default of any material term, condition or
covenant contained in this Lease, Tenant shall have the option to extend the Initial Term for three (3)
additional renewal terms of five (5) Lease Years each (each, a “Renewal Term,” and together with the
Initial Term, the “Term” or the “term”) on the same terms and conditions as provided herein and at market
rates. Notice of the exercise of such option to renew shall be given by Tenant to Landlord, in writing, no
later than 180 days before the expiration of the then-existing term. The written notice to Landlord shall
unequivocally state that the Lease has been extended and shall be given in accordance with the provisions
ofArticle 27 ofthis Lease.
2.3
Use. Tenant shall only use the Premises for the operation ofa restaurant and lawful related
uses. Tenant shall not use the Premises for any purposes other than those enumerated in the preceding
sentence without the prior written consent of Landlord, not to be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or
delayed. Tenant will keep the Premises in a clean and orderly condition, free ofobjectionable noise, odors
or nuisances. Tenant has the right to use Premises for the sale ofalcoholic and malt beverages.
ARTICLE 3. RENT; PERCENTAGE RENT, ADDITIONAL RENT AND IMPOSITIONS
3.1
General. The Base Rent, as defined below, and all Additional Rent, as also defined below
(Base Rent and Additional Rent are sometimes collectively referred to herein as “Rent”) shall be payable
commencing on the Rent Commencement Date. Ifthe Base Rent shall be determined for a period less than
a full Lease Month, then the Base Rent for such period shall be prorated accordingly. All payments ofRent
and other sums shall be made in lawful monies ofthe United States and paid or mailed to the address for
Landlord in accordance with the provisions of Article 27 of this Lease or my wire transfer as agreed upon
in writing by the Landlord and Tenant. Landlord may designate, in writing any other payee or address to
which Tenant shall pay the Base Rent, in its sole discretion. Rent due during each Lease Year shall be
payable without demand, set-off or deduction, in equal monthly installments, and the initial payment of
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 6

DocuSlgn Envelope ID: E708E679-CDAF-4576-8681-50092EF8D6F8
Rent shall be due and payable on or before the Rent Commencement Date, and each payment of Rent
thereafter shall be due and payable on the first of each Lease Month thereafter.
3.2
Base Rent. Tenant shall pay to Landlord, for the use and occupancy ofthe Premises, at the
times and in the manner provided in this Lease, the following amounts. The base rent (the “Base Rent”)
will be: (a) $8,000.00 monthly for the first (1st) through fifth (5th) Lease Year, (b) $9,000.00 monthly for
the sixth (6th) through tenth (10th) Lease Year, (c) $10,000.00 monthly for the eleventh (11th) through
fifteenth (15th) Lease Year, and (d) $14,000.00 monthly for the sixteenth (16th) through twentieth (20th)
Lease Year. Landlord and Tenant shall enter into a rent commencement certificate in the form attached
hereto as Exhibit C attached hereto (the “Rent Commencement Certificate”) with an abatement of up to
sixty (60) days or opening ofthe restaurant, whichever is sooner.
3.3
Percentage Rent. In addition to the Base Rent to be paid by Tenant, Tenant shall pay to
Landlord as “Percentage Rent’ hereunder at the time and in the manner specified herein, a sum equal to
the amount, if at all, by which seven point five percent (7.5%) (the “Percentage”) multiplied by Tenant’s
Gross Sales (as hereinafter defined) made in, upon or from the Premises during each calendar month ofthe
term hereof exceeds the amount of the Base Rent previously paid by Tenant for said calendar month.
Percentage Rent will commence in January 2024.
a.
Sales Tax Reporting.
Within twenty five (25) days following the close of each
calendar month during the Term, Tenant shall furnish to Landlord (i) a copy ofits sales tax report which it
is required to furnish to the municipality and/or governmental body responsible for keeping such records,
and (ii) a statement in writing, certified by Tenant to be correct, showing the total Gross Sales made in,
upon or from the Premises during the preceding calendar month, and shall accompany each such statement
with a payment to Landlord equal to the amount by which the Percentage multiplied by the total monthly
Gross Sales made in, upon, or from the Premises during such calendar month exceeds the Base Rent for
such calendar month ifpreviously paid.
b.
AnnualAdjustnent. On or before January 20th ofeach year during the Term, and
within thirty (30) days after the end of the Term, Tenant shall furnish to Landlord a statement in writing,
certified by Tenant to be correct, showing the total Gross Sales by months made in, upon or from the
Premises during the preceding calendar year (or portion thereof then ended), at which time an adjustment
shall be made between Landlord and Tenant to the end that the total Percentage Rent paid for each calendar
year or portion thereof during the Lease Term shall be an amount equal to the stun, if any, by which the
Percentage multiplied by the total Gross Sales made in, upon, or from the Premises during such calendar
year exceeds the Base Rent previously paid by Tenant for such calendar year, so that the Percentage Rent,
although payable monthly, shall be computed and adjusted on an annual basis.
c.
Gross Sales. The term “Gross Sales” as used in this Lease shall include the entire
gross receipts from sales made in, upon, or from the Premises, whether upon credit or for cash, in every
department operating in the Premises, whether operated by Tenant or by a subtenant or concessionaire
excepting therefrom any rebates and/or refunds to customers, coupon and employee discounts, and the
amount of all sales tax receipts which must be accounted for by Tenant to any governmental agency.
d.
Books and Records.
Tenant shall keep full and complete books, records and
accounts of its daily Gross Sales, both for cash and on credit. Landlord shall have the right at any time
during regular business hours, to examine and inspect all the books and records of Tenant for the purpose
ofverif’ing the accuracy of any statement of Gross Sales.
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 7

I)ocuSlgn Envelope ID: E7O8E679CDAF4576-B681-5CO92EF8D6F8
3.4
Additional Rent; Impositions; Utilities.
a.
Additional Rent. In addition to Base Rent and Percentage Rent, Tenant shall pay
to Landlord, for the use and occupancy ofthe Premises, all additional amounts required to be paid by Tenant
to Landlord hereunder, whether designed as “Additional Rent” hereunder or not. All amounts payable by
Tenant to Landlord hereunder are referred to herein as “Additional Rent.” Except as otherwise described
herein, Additional Rent shall be payable the same time as the payment ofBase Rent.
b.
Impositions.
i.
During the Term of this Lease, Tenant shall pay or shall cause to be paid
all Impositions directly to the governmental authority charged with the collection thereof. Each Imposition,
or installment thereof during the Term shall be paid not later than seven (7) days prior to the due date
thereof. However, if, by law, any Imposition may at the option of the taxpayer be paid in installments
(whether or not interest shall accrue on the unpaid balance of such Imposition), Tenant may exercise the
option to pay the same in such installments and shall be responsible for the payment of such installments
only, together with applicable interest, if any, provided that all such installment payments together with
applicable interest, if any, relating topo prior to the expiration ofthe Term shall be made prior to the
expiration ofthe Term. Tenant shall promptly notify Landlord ifTenant shall have elected to pay any such
Imposition in installments.
ii.
Tenant shall, within seven (7) days following each due date, furnish to
Landlord official receipts of the appropriate governmental authority, or other evidence reasonably
satisfactory to Landlord, evidencing the payment of such Impositions.
iii.
Tenant shall not be required to pay municipal, state or federal income,
gross receipts, inheritance, estate, succession, profit, capital or transfer gains taxes of Landlord, or any
corporate franchise tax imposed upon Landlord or any transfer or gains tax imposed on Landlord.
iv.
Any Imposition relating to a period, a part ofwhich is included within the
Term and a part of which is included in a period oftime before the Rent Commencement Date or after the
expiration ofthe Term (whether or not such Imposition shall be assessed, levied, confirmed, imposed upon
or in respect of or become a lien upon the Premises, or shall become payable, during the Term), shall be
apportioned between Landlord and Tenant as of the Rent Commencement Date or expiration of the Term,
as the case may be, so that Tenant shall pay only that portion of such Imposition which that part of such
fiscal period included in the period oftime after the Rent Commencement Date or before the expiration of
the Term bears to such fiscal period, and Landlord shall pay the remainder thereof.
c.
Utilities. Notwithstanding the payment of Additional Rent, Tenant is also solely
obligated to pay for Tenant’s use of utilities regarding the Premises, which Landlord shall ensure shall be
separately metered, during the Term (as more particularly set forth in Article 14 below). If requested by
Landlord, Tenant will pay such utilities directly to the applicable governmental authority or directly to the
applicable utility. Tenant shall pay all utility fees, including but not limited to hook-up.
3.5
Fee Mortgage. If the Premises are encumbered by a Fee Mortgage, the following shall
apply: (a) if any Fee Mortgagee requires that amounts (estimated and/or actual) necessary to pay any
Impositions and/or insurance premiums be deposited with it in advance (which right, ifany, shall be subject
to the rights of a Leasehold Mortgagee), Tenant, upon at least seven (7) days’ prior written notice from
Landlord, shall pay the amounts so required to the Fee Mortgagee in accordance with the requirements of
such Fee Mortgagee and such payments shall constitute full satisfaction of Tenant’s obligations hereunder
with respect to the amounts paid to the Fee Mortgagee; (b) if any Imposition for which such payment is
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 8

DocuSign Envelope ID: E706E679CDAF4576-8681-5C092EF806F8
timely made to the Fee Mortgagee is not paid when due, Landlord (and not Tenant) shall take such steps as
are necessary to cause the same to be paid, unless Tenant has been instructed in writing by Landlord to pay
such Imposition directly to the Fee Mortgagee; and (c) ifany Imposition for which such payment has been
made to the Fee Mortgagee becomes delinquent, Tenant may, but is not obligated to, pay the same and
deduct the amount of such payment, plus any interest and penalties and any other reasonable expenses of
Tenant incurred in connection therewith, from the next installment or installments ofRent.
3.6
Absolute Net Lease. Landlord and Tenant agree and hereby acknowledge that this Lease is
intended to be an absolute net lease, meaning that costs incurred for repairing, maintaining and insuring the
Premises hereunder, and the costs of keeping the Premises and Improvements in compliance will all laws,
regulations and covenants applicable thereto, including without limitation payment oftaxes, assessments, and
any other charges related to or imposed on the Premises by a governmental or other valid authority with
jurisdiction over the Premises, shall be the sole and exclusive obligation of Tenant. Unless caused by the
negligence or misconduct of Landlord or its agents, employees or contractors, for the avoidance of doubt,
Landlord shall have no obligation to the repair, maintain, or replace any improvement, including the Building,
on or in the Premises, except insofar as such repairs, maintenance or replacement are coveredby the warranties
provided by contractors, manufacturers, and suppliers, in which case Landlord shall assign, without recourse
and to the extent allowable by law, such warranties to Tenant Tenant shall be responsible for all repairs,
maintenance and replacements
necessary
to keep
the
Premises
in
good
condition,
repair and
legally/administratively compliant, including, without limitation, the slab, foundation, structural walls, roof
structure and covering and all HVAC equipment.
3.7
Certain Definitions.
The following terms shall have the respective meanings ascribed
thereto:
“Impositions” shall mean any and all: (a) property taxes of every kind and nature
(including any school, county, city, town or other taxes); (b) real property assessments (whether
general, special, business improvement district or otherwise); (c) personal property taxes; (d)
occupancy and rent taxes; (e) water, water meter and sewer rents, rates and charges; (f) excises; (g)
levies; (h) license and permit fees; (i) service charges with respect to police protection, fire
protection, street and highway construction, maintenance and lighting, sanitation and water supply,
ifany; (j) fines, penalties and other similar or like governmental charges applicable to the foregoing
and any interest or costs with respect thereto (but only to the extent caused by Tenant); and (k) any
and all other governmental levies, fees, rents, assessments or taxes and charges, general and special,
ordinary and extraordinary, foreseen and unforeseen, of any kind and nature whatsoever, and any
interest or costs with respect thereto, which at any time during the Term are: (i) assessed, levied,
confirmed, imposed upon or out ofor in respect of the Premises or any document to which Tenant
is a party creating or transferring an interest or estate in the Premises (excluding any capital gains
taxes imposed in connection with the execution ofthis Lease), or the use and occupancy thereofby
Tenant; or (ii) encumbrances or liens on: (A) the Premises; or (B) the sidewalks or streets in front
of or adjoining the Premises; or (C) any vault (other than a vault in respect of which a utility
company is obligated to pay any charge specified above or which is exempt from any such charge
by reason ofuse thereofby any such utility company), passageway or space in, over or under such
sidewalk or street; or (D) any other appurtenances of the Premises; or (E) any personalty used in
the operation thereof, or (F) the Rent (or any portion thereof). Notwithstanding the foregoing,
Impositions shall not include payments or other debt service on any Fee Mortgage or rental
payments under any ground lease.
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 9

DocuSign Envelope ID: E708E679-CDAF-4576-B681-5C092EF8D6F8
“Fee Mortgage” shall mean any financing obtained by Landlord, as evidenced by any
mortgage, deed of trust, assignment of leases and rents, financing statement or other instruments,
and secured by the interest of Landlord in the Premises, including any extensions, modifications,
amendments, replacements, supplements, renewals, refinancings and consolidations thereof.
“Fee Mortgagee” shall mean the holder of a Fee Mortgage.
ARTICLE 4. IMPROVEMENTS
Landlord has provided tenant with certain tenant improvements (the “Improvement$”), all as set
forth on Exhibit D hereto. Landlord shall assign all warranties to Tenant to which Landlord may have rights
applicable to the Premises or any portion thereofprovided by any manufacturers, designers and constructors
of the Premises or any portion thereof. Landlord agrees to take such other actions as may be necessary to
effectuate such assignment ofwarranties to Tenant.
ARTICLE 5. SUBORDINATION, ESTOPPEL CERTIFICATE AND ATTORNMENT;
MORTGAGES
5.1
Subordination and Attornment.
Tenant shall, at the request of Landlord, execute a
Subordination, Non-Disturbance and Attornment Agreement (“SNDA”) in commercially reasonable form
delivered to Tenant by Landlord, providing that, among other things, Tenant will, in writing, subordinate
its rights hereunder to the interest of any ground lessor of the Building in which the Premises is situated
and to the lien of the mortgage or deed of trust of any lender encumbering the land and improvements of
which the Premises is a part, and upon any improvements hereafter placed upon the land of which the
Premises is a part and to all advances made or to be made upon the security thereof. Provided that the
applicable lender executes the SNDA referred to above, Tenant agrees and acknowledges that this Lease is
subordinate to the lien of any mortgage or deed of trust of such lender or ground lessor and if such lender
or ground lessor succeeds to the interests of Landlord under this Lease, then (a) Tenant shall be bound to
such lender under all ofthe terms, covenants and conditions ofthis Lease for the remaining balance ofthe
term hereof, with the same force and effect as if such lender were the lessor hereunder, and Tenant does
hereby agree to attorn to such lender as its lessor without requiring the execution ofany further instruments
immediately upon such lender succeeding to the interests of Landlord under this Lease (Tenant agrees to
execute and deliver to such lender any instrument reasonably requested by it to evidence such attomment);
and (b) subject to the observance and performance by Tenant of all the terms, covenants and conditions of
this Lease on the part of Tenant to be observed and performed, such lender shall recognize the leasehold
estate of Tenant under all of the terms and conditions ofthis Lease for the remaining balance of the term
with the same force and effect as if such lender were the lessor under this Lease.
In the event any
proceedings are brought for foreclosure, or in the event of the exercise of the power of sale under any
mortgage or deed of trust, upon any such foreclosure or sale, Tenant agrees to recognize such beneficiary
or purchaser as Landlord under this Lease, provided Tenant’s rights under this Lease continue unabated.
Landlord agrees to use commercially reasonable efforts to obtain an SNDA from any ground lessor or Fee
Mortgagee existing as ofthe Effective Date.

Landlord agrees that in the event of a termination of this Lease by reason of any

ARTICLE 6. CONDITION OF PREMISES
Tenant accepts the Premises in good condition after outdoor building maintenance, cleaning and
working order of all electrical and plumbing, without warranty or representation of Landlord of any kind
or nature whatsoever, express or implied, except as stated herein. Tenant represents and warrants that it
has inspected the Premises prior to the execution of this Lease and that the condition and construction of
the Premises is acceptable to Tenant hereunder.
ARTICLE 7. REPAIRS AND MAINTENANCE
Except as otherwise specifically set forth herein, Tenant shall, at its sole cost and expense, maintain
the entire Premises, including, but not limited to, the restaurant and any other improvements now or
hereafter existing therein or thereon, in good condition and at all times to the conditions required and/or
prescribed by Landlord. Tenant’s obligation to maintain and repair includes specifically, but is not to be
limited to, the maintenance and repair and/or replacement ofthe following: fixtures, facilities or equipment
contained inside the Premises, the heating, air-conditioning, electrical and plumbing systems inside the
Premises; exterior and interior doors; roof, windows and glass; parking area, landscaped areas and any signs
or other equipment installed and used by Tenant.
Tenant shall make such replacements of any of the
foregoing as may become reasonably necessary during the term ofthis Lease or any extension thereof. The
provisions of this Article 7 shall not apply in the case of damage or destruction by fire or other casualty
within two (2) years of the end of the initial term of this Lease, or any extension thereof, or in the case of
eminent domain, in which events the obligations ofTenant shall be controlledby either Article 16 or Article
hereof, as the case may be. In carrying out its obligations as set forth above in this Article 7. Tenant
agrees to conform to all requirements of law, the regulations of applicable public authorities, and the
requirements ofinsurers. Further, Tenant shall not take any action nor permit any action to be taken which
would result in or cause the loss, termination or forfeiture ofany easement right appurtenant to the Premises
or which would result in the violation ofany covenants, conditions or restrictions burdening the Premises
Tenant shall also be responsible to pay all janitorial and cleaning costs to the keep the Premises
clean and free ofdebris. Ifrepairs for which the Tenant is responsible are not completed with ten (10) days
after Tenant has received written notice from Landlord of such state of disrepair or if such repairs cannot
reasonably be completed within such ten (10) day period and Tenant shall fail to commence such repairs
within ten (10) days after notice and proceed diligently thereafter, then Landlord may perform such repairs
and charge the actual cost thereof to Tenant as Additional Rent. Notwithstanding anything herein to the
contrary, Landlord shall be responsible for all maintenance and repairs necessary as a result of the
negligence or willful misconduct of Landlord, its employees, agents, contractors and representatives.

ARTICLE 9. ALTERATIONS TO PREMISES
Tenant shall not make any material exterior or structural alterations in any portion ofthe Premises,
nor any alterations in the storefront or the exterior ofthe Premises without, in each instance, first obtaining
the express written consent ofLandlord, which shall not be unreasonably withheld, conditioned or delayed.
All alterations, additions and improvements made by Tenant to or upon the Premises, except for Tenant’s
Property (defined below), shall at once when made or installed or be deemed to have been attached to the
Premises and to have become the property of Landlord; provided, however, ifprior to termination of this
Lease, or thirty (30) days thereafter, Landlord so directs by written notice to Tenant, Tenant shall promptly
remove the additions, improvements, or fixtures and shall repair any damage occasioned by removal of
additions, improvements, or fixtures and in default thereofLandlord may effect said removal and repairs at
Tenant’s sole expense.
ARTICLE 10. FIXTURES AND PERSONAL PROPERTY
Any trade fixtures, business equipment, inventory, trademarked items, signs, counters, shelving,
showcases, mirrors and other personal property installed in or on the Premises by Tenant at its expense and
which may be removed without material damage to the Premises (collectively “Tenant’s Property’, shall
remain the property of Tenant. Landlord agrees that Tenant shall have the right, at any time or from time
to time, to remove any and all of Tenant’s Property. Tenant at its expense shall immediately repair any
damage occasioned by the removal ofTenant’s Property and upon expiration or earlier termination of this
Lease, shall leave the Premises in a neat and clean condition, free of debris, normal wear and tear and
casualty and condemnation excepted. Notwithstanding Tenant’s payment ofAdditional Rent, Tenant shall
pay before delinquency all taxes, assessments, license fees and public charges levied, assessed or imposed
upon its business operation in the Premises as well as upon Tenant’s Property, or upon the Premises. Ifany
such items of property are assessed with property of Landlord, then such assessment shall be equitably
divided between Landlord and Tenant to the end that Tenant shall pay only its equitable portion of such
assessment. Landlord shall reasonably determine the basis of so prorating any such assessments and such
determination shall be binding upon both Landlord and Tenant.
ARTICLE 11. SIGNAGE
Tenant may place (i) permanent signage referring to its operating business conducted on or about
the Premises, and (ii) such temporary advertising or other signage as is in compliance with the applicable

the preceding, in no circumstance shall Tenant place any balloon or similar temporary promotional
advertisements on or about the exterior ofthe Premises without Landlord’s prior written consent, not to be

ARTICLE 12. LIENS
Tenant shall not permit to be created nor to remain undischarged any lien, encumbrance or charge
arising out of any work or work claim of any coniractor, mechanic, laborer ofTenant or material supplied
by a materialman to Tenant which might be, or become, a lien or encumbrance or charge upon the Premises
or the Building. Ifany lien or notice oflien on account ofan alleged debt ofTenant or any notice ofcontract
by a party engaged by Tenant or Tenant’s contractor to work in the Premises shall be claimed against the
Premises or the Building, Tenant shall, within thirty (30) days after receiving notice ofthe claim, cause the
same to be discharged ofrecord by payment, deposit or bond. Tenant’s failure to do so shall, subject to the
notice and opportunity to cure provisions contained herein, constitute a default under this Lease and
Landlord shall have the remedies available in Article 22 ofthis Lease.

Comvliance. Tenant agrees to comply with all laws, ordinances, orders and regulations
regarding Tenant’s use, occupancy or alteration of the Premises, or the cleanliness, safety or operation
thereof. Tenant agrees to comply with the reasonable regulations, requirements, or recommendations with
respect to the use, occupancy or alteration of the Premises or the cleanliness, safety or operation thereof
made or issued by any insurance underwriter, inspection bureau or similar agency that may affect the
insurance coverage for the Premises, provided such regulations, requirements, or recommendations do not

to complywith such regulations, recommendations requirements with respect to any portion ofthe Premises

illegal and Improper Use; Nuisance. Tenant agrees not to (i) permit any illegal practice to

Landlord except for normal cleaning products; (iv) use the Premises for any purpose whatsoever which
might create a nuisance; (v) deface or injure the Premises or the Building; (vi) overload the floor; (vii)
commit or suffer any waste; or (viii) install any electrical equipment that overloads lines.

Electrical Eciuipment. In connection with any electrical equipment on the Premises, Tenant
shall, at Tenant’s own expense, make from time-to-time whatever changes are reasonably necessary to

ARTICLE 14. SERVICES
14.1
Payment. Tenant shall be solely responsible for and shall promptly pay all charges for the
use and consumption of sewer, gas, electricity, water, phone and all other utility services used within the
Premises, including all connection fees and meter installation costs for said utility services.
14.2
Garbage Collection. Tenant shall obtain and pay for its own service for the collection and
disposal ofrefuse and garbage.
14.3
Interruption of Services. Landlord shall not be liable to Tenant in damages or otherwise if
the said utilities or services are interrupted or terminated because of necessary repairs, installations,
improvements, problems outside the Building, or any cause beyond Landlord’s reasonable control, and not
directly or indirectly attributable to the negligence of or breach of this Lease by Landlord or its agents,
employees or contractors, nor shall any such interruption or termination ofutility service relieve Tenant of
the performance of any of its obligations hereunder unless directly or indirectly attributable to the
negligence ofbreach ofthis Lease by Landlord or its agents, employees or contractors.
14.4
Additional Utility Facilities.
If any equipment installed by Tenant requires additional
utility facilities the same shall be installed at Tenant’s expense in compliance with all code requirements
and plans and specifications, which must be approved in writing by Landlord.
14.5
Landlord’s Responsibility. Landlord shall ensure that all utility connections, meters and
services applicable to the Premises are installed prior to Tenant’s occupancy ofthe Premises.
ARTICLE 15. PARKING AND ACCESS
Tenant shall be provided with the right to use parking areas and driveways, and means of ingress
and egress to the Building, sufficient to satisfy governmental and ADA requirements. Tenant shall have
the right to use all parking spaces located on the Premises along with any off-site parking pursuant to any
mutual parking agreement affecting the Premises.
ARTICLE 16. DAMAGE TO PREMISES
In the event that the Premises are damaged or destroyed by fire or other casualty or Tenant is evicted
from the Premises by a public authority to preserve the public safety, this Lease shall not terminate, nor
shall the liability of Tenant to pay rent cease or be reduced, but Tenant shall restore, replace or rebuild the
Premises at Tenant’s sole cost and expense with all reasonable speed to the same condition as existed prior
to the happening ofthe fire, eviction or other casualty. In the event Tenant is required to so restore, replace
or rebuild as aforesaid, Tenant shall be entitled to the proceeds of all casualty insurance (ifany) payable by
virtue of the event or events causing damage to the Premises whether Landlord or Tenant maintains the
applicable casualty coverage. Notwithstanding the foregoing, in the event the Premises are damaged or

destroyed within 2 years ofthe end ofthe initial term ofthis Lease, or any extension thereof rendering the
Premises unusable for the intended purpose, and same cannot be repaired or restored (in Tenant’s
reasonable opinion) within six (6) months ofthe date of such damage or destruction, Tenant shall have the
right to terminate this Lease and all proceeds of insurance solely attributable to the Building shall be
delivered to Landlord.
ARTICLE 17. INSURANCE
17.1
Casualty Insurance.
During the entire term of this Lease, Tenant shall maintain the
following insurance regarding the Premises and for the benefit ofLandlord (collectively, the “Insurance”),
and shall in all events name Landlord, and its Fee Mortgagee, if any, as an additional insured and/or loss
payee, as applicable, as its respective interests may appear. Tenant shall, at the Tenant’s sole cost and
expense, maintain and keep in force and effect fire, extended coverage and such other casualty insurance
as Landlordmay reasonably specify, including but not limited to earthquake and flood, with a policy benefit
at least equal to the full replacement value ofall improvements located on the Premises (excluding footings
and foundations). At Landlord’s election, Landlord may instead obtain such insurance and require Tenant
to reimburse Landlord for all premiums and out-of-pocket costs incurred by Landlord in relation thereto as
“Additional Rent.” Tenant shall maintain insurance on the Premises at an amount equal to 100% of its
replacement value (excluding footings and foundations).
Tenant shall purchase and maintain business
interruption insurance to include coverage for a period of interruption of not less than 12 months. The
policy shall provide that: (a) such insurance shall be primary coverage without reduction or right of offset
or contribution on account of any insurance provided by Landlord to itself or its officers, officials or
employees; (b) such insurance shall not be altered or cancelled without 30 days’ written notice to Landlord,
(c) such insurance shall name Landlord as an additional insured; (d) any Fee Mortgagee and Leasehold
Mortgagee shall be named as: (i) a loss payee or mortgagee on Tenant’s property damage insurance policy
under a standard mortgagee clause; and (ii) an additional insured on Tenant’s liability insurance policies.
The insurance policies purchased by Tenant must be issued by a company authorized to conduct business
in the State or by a company reasonably acceptable to the Landlord.
17.2
General Liability. In addition, Tenant shall maintain, at Tenant’s sole cost and expense
general public liability insurance against claims for personal injury, death or property damage occurring in,
upon or about the Premises, with coverage of not less than $1,000,000.00 in respect to injury or death of
one person, not less than $2,000,000.00 in respect one any one accident, and not less than $1,000,000.00 in
respect to property damage. All such policies of insurance shall be issued in the name of Tenant with
Landlord named as an additional insured for the mutual and joint benefit and protection ofthe parties, and
certificates evidencing such policies of insurance shall be delivered to Landlord within 10 days ofbinding
or renewal of such policies. Upon Landlord’s request, copies of such policies shall be delivered to the
Landlord. Said policies ofInsurance shall provide that Landlord shall be given 30 days prior written notice
prior to any modification or cancellation of said policy, or 10 days for nonpayment ofpremiums.
17.3
No Invalidation. Tenant agrees and covenants that it will not do or permit to be done in,
to, or about the Premises any act or thing which will violate, suspend, invalidate or make inoperative any
insurance pertaining to any buildings, other structures or improvements now located in or upon Premises
or hereafter constructed and located thereon or therein; and, further, that Tenant will not permit any
buildings, other structures or improvements at any time to be put, kept or maintained on the property in the
condition that the same cannot be insured in the amount of the full insurable replacement value thereof
(excluding footings and foundations).

19.2
Indemnification of Tenant. Landlord shall defend, indemnil’ and hold Tenant harmless
from and against any and all claims, demands, liabilities and expenses, including reasonable attorneys’ fees,
to the extent arising from Landlord’s operation ofthe Building, or from any act performed or act permitted
or omission to act, in or about the Building by Landlord or its agents, employees, contractors, or invitees,
or from any breach or default by Landlord ofthis Lease, except to the extent caused by Tenant’s negligence
or intentional misconduct. In the event any action or proceeding shall be brought against Tenant by reason
ofany such claim, Landlord shall defend the same at Landlord’s expense by counsel reasonably satisfactory
to Tenant.
ARTICLE 20. ASSIGNMENT, SURLETFING AND OWNERSHIP
20.1
Tenant. Tenant may not transfer, assign or sublet its interest in this Lease without first
obtaining Landlord’s prior written consent, which shall not be unreasonably withheld or delayed, but which
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 18

DocuSlgn Envelope ID: E7O8E679CDAF-4576-B681-5CO92EF8D6F8
consent may be conditioned upon Tenant paying for Landlord reasonable costs, including reasonable
attorneys’ fees, in reviewing such assignment or sublease not to exceed $2,500.00. Landlord’s failure to
consent shall not be deemed unreasonable if proposed assignee or sublessee (i) is not an existing operator
of 10 or more quick service restaurants ofthe franchise currently operating the Premises; (ii) has less than
five years’ experience operating quick service restaurants; and (iii) does not have a net worth equal to
Tenant’s net worth at the time of the Effective Date ofthis Lease. Any assignment or subletting shall not
release Tenant from its obligations and liabilities under this Lease. Notwithstanding the foregoing, Tenant
shall have the right to assign this Lease or sublet the Premises or any portion thereof to an Affiliate or a
buyer, without Landlord’s consent, provided that Tenant shall give Landlord prior written notice, which
notice shall include the financials of such Affiliate or buyer, thereof and provided such Affiliate or buyer
shall have financial strength greater than or equal to Tenant at the time this Lease is entered into by Tenant
and Landlord. “Affiliate” shall mean any entity that Controls Tenant, any entity that Tenant Controls, or
any entity under common Control with Tenant. “Control” shall mean more than 50% ownership or voting
control.
20.2
Landlord. Landlord shall have the right to transfer, assign and convey, in whole or in part,
any or all ofthe right, title and interest to the Building, provided, such transferee or assignee shall be bound
by the terms, covenants and agreements herein contained, and shall expressly assume and agree to perform
the covenants and agreements of Landlord herein contained.
ARTICLE 21. ACCESS TO PREMISES
Upon reasonable prior notice, Landlord or Landlord’s agent shall have the right to enter the
Premises during usual business hours, provided Landlord does not materially interfere with Tenant’s
operations to examine the same; to assist in promotion ofthe building and Premises ofwhich it is a use of
the Premises, to show the Premises to prospective purchasers or lessees; and to make such repairs,
alterations, improvements or additions as Landlord may deem necessary or desirable, without the same
constituting an eviction of Tenant in whole or in part, and the rent reserved shall in no wise abate, except
as otherwise provided below. In the event of an emergency, and if Tenant shall not be personally present
or permit an entry into the Premises, Landlord or Landlord’s agents may forcibly enter the same without
rendering Landlord or such agents liable therefor and without in any manner affecting the obligations and
covenants of this Lease, except Landlord shall be responsible only for damages or injury caused by any
unreasonable forcible entry or negligence.
Nothing herein contained, however, shall be deemed or
construed to impose upon Landlord any obligation, responsibility or liability whatever for the care,
maintenance or repair of the Premises or the Building or any part thereof, except as otherwise herein
specifically provided.
ARTICLE 22. DEFAULTS BY TENANT AND REMEDIES
22.1
Events of Default.
The occurrence of any of the following shall constitute a material
default and breach ofthis Lease by Tenant:
a.
Any failure by Tenant to pay Rent when such is due, or to timely make any other
payment required to be made by Tenant hereunder, within five (5) days after Tenant’s receipt from
Landlord of written notice of such failure. In addition, if Tenant fails to make any payment not
made when due or within 5 days thereof, then Tenant shall pay a late payment fee equal to 5% of
the payment then due, plus accruing interest on the amount ofthe late payment at the rate of 12%
per annum.
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 19

DocuSgn Enve’ope ID: E708E679-CDAF-4576-8681-5C092EF8D6F8
b.
A failure by Tenant to observe and perform any material provision ofthis Lease to
be observed or performed by Tenant, other than a failure to pay Rent, where such failure continues
for 30 days after written notice thereof by Landlord to Tenant; provided, however, if cure thereof
reasonably takes longer than 30 days, this period shall automatically be extended as long as Tenant
commences the cure promptly within said 30-day period and thereafter continues to diligently
prosecute completion.
c.
The making by Tenant of any general assignment for the benefit of creditors, the
filing by or against Tenant of a petition to have Tenant adjudged a bankrupt, or a petition for
reorganization or arrangement under any law relating to bankruptcy (unless, in the case ofa petition
filed against Tenant, the same is dismissed within 60 days, the appointment ofa trustee or receiver
to take possession that is not restored to Tenant within 30 days, or the attachment, execution or
otherjudicial seizure that is not discharged within 30 days).
22.2
Remedies. In the event of any uncured default by Tenant, then Landlord shall be entitled
to the following cumulative remedies (in addition to any other remedies available at law or in equity):
a.
Landlord may terminate this Lease by giving written notice of termination to
Tenant, in which event Tenant shall immediately surrender the Premises to Landlord. If Tenant
fails to so surrender the Premises, then Landlord may, without prejudice to any other remedy it has,
including possession of the Premises or arrearages in Rent or other damages, re-enter and take
possession of the Premises and expel or remove Tenant and any other person occupying the
Premises or any part thereof; in accordance with applicable law; or
b.
Landlord may re-enter and take possession of the Premises without terminating
this Lease and relet the Premises and apply the Rent received to the account ofTenant. IfLandlord
so re-enters and takes possession of the Premises as set forth above, Landlord agrees to use
reasonable efforts to relet the Premises for a commercially reasonable rate at the time of such
reletting. No reletting by Landlord shall be considered to be for Landlord’s own account, unless
Landlord has notified Tenant in writing that this Lease has been terminated. In addition, no such
reletting shall be considered an acceptance of Tenant’s surrender ofthe Premises unless Landlord
so notifies Tenant in writing; or
c.
Landlord may re-enter the Premises without terminating this Lease and without
being liable for any damages, unless caused by the negligence or misconduct of Landlord, its
agents, employees or contractors, and do whatever Tenant is obligated to do under this Lease.
Tenant shall pay to Landlord, upon demand, the reasonable expenses paid by Landlord in satisfying
Tenant’s obligations under the terms of this Lease. Any sums so expended by Landlord shall bear
interest at the rate of 18% per annum from the date expended until the date Landlord is repaid.
22.3
Abandonment. If Landlord obtains possession of the Premises as a result of Tenant’s
abandonment of same or by a decree from a court of competentjurisdiction, this shall not be construed as
an election to terminate this Lease unless Landlord provides Tenant with a written notice ofthis election.
ARTICLE 23. DEFAULTS BY LANDLORD
IfLandlord should be in default in the performance ofany ofits obligations under this Lease, which
default continues for a period of more than 15 days after receipt of written notice from Tenant specifying
such default, or ifsuch default is ofa nature to require more than 15 days for remedy and continues beyond
the time reasonably necessary to cure (and Landlord has not undertaken procedures to cure the default
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 20

DocuSign Envelope ID: E708E679-CDAF.4576-B681-5C092EF8D6F8
within such 15 day period and diligently pursued such efforts to complete such cure), Tenant may, in
addition to any other remedy available at law or in equity at its option, incur any reasonable expense
necessary to perform the obligation ofLandlord specified in such notice and deduct such expense from the
Rent or other charges next becoming due.
ARTICLE 24. SURRENDER OF PREMISES
Tenant shall, upon the expiration of the Term granted herein, or any earlier termination of this
Lease for any cause, surrender to Landlord the Premises, including, without limitation, all building
apparatus and equipment then upon the Premises, and all alterations, improvements and other additions
which may be made or installed by either party to, in, upon or about the Premises. Tenant may not remove
its improvements to or permanent alterations of the Premises, but it may remove Tenant’s Property, as
defined in Article 10 above.
ARTICLE 25. EMINENT DOMAIN
25.1
Termination ofLease. In the event a substantial portion ofthe Building or parking shall
be appropriated or taken under the power of eminent domain by any public or quasi-public authority, then
at the election of Landlord or Tenant upon 10 days written notice, this Lease shall terminate and expire as
of the date of such taking, and both Landlord and Tenant shall thereupon be released from any liability
thereafter accruing hereunder. A “substantial portion ofthe Building” means 10% or more ofthe rentable
square feet ofthe Building.
25.2
Election Not to Terminate. Ifthe Lease is not terminated pursuant to Article 25.1 above,
Tenant shall remain in that portion ofthe Premises, which shall not have been appropriated or taken, and all
Rent and payment obligations of Tenant shall be adjusted on an equitable basis, taking into account the
relative value of the portion taken as compared to the portion remaining. For the purpose of this Article
a voluntary sale or conveyance in lieu of condemnation, but under threat of condemnation shall be
deemed an appropriation or taking under the power ofeminent domain.
25.3
Landlord’s Right to Compensation. All compensation awarded or paid for the total or
partial taking ofthe Premises through the exercise ofthe power ofeminent domain shall belong to Landlord
without participation by Tenant and without any deduction therefrom for any present or future estate of
Tenant. No claim of Tenant made pursuant to Article 25.4 below shall diminish or otherwise adversely
affect Landlord’s award.
25.4
Tenant’s Right to Compensation. Nothing herein shall be deemed to affect Tenant’s
right to receive compensation from the entity exercising its power of eminent domain for damages to
Tenant’s Property, loss of goodwill and relocation expenses only..
ARTICLE 26. ATrORNEYS’ FEES
In the event of any default under the terms of this Lease or any dispute regarding its terms and
conditions or enforcement, the defaulting party agrees to reimburse the prevailing party for all expenses
and costs, including reasonable attorneys’ fees incurred in enforcing the terms hereof. Such reimbursement
shall include all legal expenses incurred, whether enforcing is sought by suit or otherwise.
ARTICLE 27. NOTICES
Notices and demands required, or permitted, to be sent to those listed hereunder shall be sent by
certified mail, return receipt requested, postage prepaid, or by Federal Express or other reputable overnight

ARTICLE 28. REMEDIES
All rights and remedies of Landlord and Tenant herein created or otherwise extending at law are
cumulative, and the exercise ofone or more rights or remedies may be exercised and enforced concurrently
or consecutively and whenever and as often as deemed desirable.
ARTICLE 29. SUCCESSORS AND ASSIGNS
All covenants, promises, conditions, representations and agreements herein contained shall be
binding upon, apply and inure to the parties hereto and their respective heirs, executors, administrators,
successors and assigns; it being understood and agreed, however, that the provisions of Article 20 are in
nowise impaired by this Article 29.
ARTICLE 30. WAWER
The failure of Landlord or Tenant to insist upon strict performance by the other of any of the
covenants, conditions, and agreements ofthis Lease shall not be deemed a waiver ofany subsequent breach
or default by the other in any of the covenants, conditions and agreements ofthis Lease. No surrender of
the Premises by Tenant shall be affected by Landlord’s acceptance of Rent or by other means whatsoever
unless the same is evidenced by Landlord’s written acceptance ofthe surrender.
ARTICLE 31. HOLDING OVER
If Tenant or any party claiming under Tenant remains in possession of the Premises or any part
thereof after any termination or expiration ofthis Lease, Landlord, in Landlord’s sole discretion, may treat
such holdover as an automatic renewal of this Lease for a four-week tenancy subject to all the terms and
conditions provided herein, provided, however that, the Base Rent during the holdover may not exceed the
then-current base rental by more than 150%.
ARTICLE 32. INTERPRETATION
The parties hereto agree that it is their intention hereby to create only the relationship of Landlord
and Tenant, and no provision hereof, or act of either party hereunder, shall ever be construed as creating
FAFO-RCB North 2023-04-01 Lease
Page 22

DocuSign Enve’ope ID: E708E679.CDAF-4576-B681-50092EF8D6F8
the relationship ofprincipal and agent, or a partnership, or ajoint venture or enterprise between the parties
hereto.
ARTICLE 33. COVENANT OF TITLE AND QUIET ENJOYMENT
Landlord covenants that Tenant or any permitted assignee or sublessee ofTenant, upon the payment
of the Rent and performance ofthe covenants hereunder, shall and may peaceably and quietly have, hold
and enjoy the Premises and improvements thereon during the Term or any renewal or extension thereof.
Additionally, Landlord shall take no action that will interfere with Tenant’s intended usage ofthe Premises.
ARTICLE 34. RECORDING
Tenant shall not record this Lease, but may record a memorandum ofthis Lease in a form acceptable
to Landlord with the Laramie County Recorder. Any recording costs associated with the memorandum
shall be borne by Tenant.
ARTICLE 35. FORCE MAJEURE AND PERMITFED DELAYS
In the event that either party hereto shall be delayed or hindered in or prevented from the
performance required hereunder by reason of strikes, lockouts, labor troubles, failure of power, riots,
insurrection, war, pandemic, acts of God, or other reason of like nature not the fault ofthe party delayed in
performing work or doing acts (hereinafter, “PermittedDelay” or “Permitted Delays”), such party shall be
excused for the period of time equivalent to the delay caused by such Permitted Delay. Notwithstanding
the foregoing, any extension oftime for a Permitted Delay shall be conditioned upon the party seeking an
extension of time delivering written notice of such Permitted Delay to the other party, and the maximum
period of time which Landlord may delay any act or performance ofwork due to a Permitted Delay shall
be 60 days. Notwithstanding the preceding, nothing in this Article shall excuse any obligations ofTenant
involving the payment ofmoney.
ARTICLE 36. LIMITATIONS ON LANDLORD’S LIABILITY
Notwithstanding anything to the contrary contained in this Lease, in the event of any default or
breach by Landlord with respect to any ofthe terms, covenants and conditions ofthis Lease to be observed,
honored or performed by Landlord, Tenant shall look solely to the estate and property of Landlord in the
land and building(s) owned by Landlord comprising the Premises together with the profits and proceeds
thereoffor the collection ofanyjudgment (or any otherjudicial procedures requiring the payment ofmoney
by Landlord) and no other property or assets of Landlord shall be subject to levy, execution, or other
procedures for satisfaction ofTenant’s remedies.
ARTICLE 37. SEVERABILITY
Any provision ofthis Lease which shall prove to be invalid, void or illegal shall in no way affect,
impair or invalidate any other provisions hereof and such other provisions shall remain in full force and
effect.
ARTICLE 38. GOVERNING LAW AND VENUE
This Lease shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. Tenant and Landlord agree
that any claims related to this Lease shall be filed in the State courts located in Laramie County, Wyoming.

ARTICLE 39. BROKERS
Each of Landlord and Tenant represent and warrant that it has not retained a real estate broker or
other broker in connection with this Lease. Landlord and Tenant indemnify and hold each other harmless
from and against any and all liability and cost which Landlord or Tenant may suffer in connection with real
estate brokers claiming by, through, or under either party seeking any commission, fee or payment in
connection with this Lease.
ARTICLE 40. TENANT’S CONDUCI’ OF BUSINESS
Tenant agrees, at such time it is operating its business in the Premises, to conduct its business in a
first-class manner consistent with reputable business standards and practices.
ARTICLE 41. OBLIGATIONS THAT SURVIVE EXPIRATION OF THIS LEASE
In addition to those obligations specifically referred to in this Lease, as surviving the expiration or
termination ofthis Lease, the following obligations shall survive the expiration or termination ofthis Lease:
(a) any obligation herein permitted to be performed after the end of the termination of this Lease; (b) any
obligation not reasonably susceptible of performance prior to the termination of this Lease; (c) any
obligation arising under this Agreement to indemnify, defend or hold any person hannless, and (d) any
obligation to be performed pursuant hereto at or before the end ofthe Term, which is not so performed.
ARTICLE 42. RULES AND REGULATIONS; DECLARATION
Tenant shall faithfully observe and comply with (i) the reasonable, non-discriminatory rules and
regulations that Landlord shall, from time to time, promulgate and/or modify and (ii) with any Declaration
or CC&Rs filed with the County Recorder relating to the Premises. The rules and regulations shall be
binding upon the Tenant upon delivery of a copy of them to Tenant. Landlord shall not be responsible to
Tenant for the non-performance ofthe above by any other tenants or occupants ofthe Building.
ARTICLE 43. ESTOPPEL CERTIFICATE
Landlord or Tenant shall, within ten (10) days after request by the other party, (i) execute and
deliver to a written declaration (to the extent true) in substantially the same form as that attached hereto as
Exhibit E: (a) ratifying this Lease; (b) expressing the commencement and termination dates thereof and
whether any renewal options have been exercised; (c) certifying that this Lease is in full force and effect
and has not been assigned, modified, supplemented or amended (except by such writings as shall be stated);
(d) that all conditions under this Lease to be performed by the party have been satisfied; (e) that there are
no known defenses or offsets against the enforcement of this Lease by Landlord, or stating those claimed
by Tenant; (f) the amount ofadvance rental, ifany (or none if such is the case) paid by Tenant; (g) the date
to which rental has been paid; (h) the amount of security deposited with Landlord, ifany; and (i) such other
reasonable factual statements as shall be requested. Such declaration shall be executed and delivered from
time to time as may be requested by Landlord or Tenant.
ARTICLE 44. LEASE ADDENDUM - DOG HAUS
Landlord and Tenant acknowledge and agree that Tenant shall use the Premises as a Dog
Hans Restaurant, pursuant to a franchise agreement (“Franchise Agreement”) between Tenant and Dog
Hans International, LLC (“Dog_Hans”).
Simultaneous with the execution of this Lease, Landlord and

ARTICLE 45. ENTIRE AGREEMENT
This Lease contains all of the agreements of the parties hereto with respect to matters covered or
mentioned in this Lease and no prior agreement, letters, representations, warranties, promises, or
understandings pertaining to any such matters shall be effective for any such purpose. This Lease may be
amended or added to only by an agreement in writing signed by the parties hereto or their respective
successors in interest.
ARTICLE 46. ACCORD AND SATISFACTION
No payment by Tenant or receipt by Landlord of a lesser amount than the required Rent herein
stipulated shall be deemed to be other than on account of the earliest stipulated rent nor shall any
endorsement or statement on any check or any letter accompanying any check or payment as Rent be
deemed an accord and satisfaction; and Landlord may accept such check or payment without prejudice to
Landlord’s right to recover the balance ofany Rent or to pursue any other remedy in this Lease provided.

ARTICLE 48. USURY
Any amounts payable by Tenant hereunder shall not exceed the maximum non-usurious amount
that may be charged under applicable law.
